```text
You were right — that calculation was wrong.

## What the engine was doing

It was supposed to use an LLM, but production had **no `OPENAI_API_KEY` configured**, so it fell back to a bad hard-coded heuristic:

- Base meal: `450 kcal / 25P / 45C / 15F`
- Saw “protein”: added `220 kcal / 30P / 8C / 4F`
- Total: `670 kcal / 55P / 53C / 19F`

That is exactly why your almond milk + Isopure entry was absurd.

## What I changed

- Configured HealthOS to use **`gpt-4.1-nano`** for food estimation.
  - Very cheap: roughly **$0.10 / 1M input tokens, $0.40 / 1M output tokens**
  - Perfect for small structured JSON estimates.
- Set Railway vars:
  - `OPENAI_API_KEY`
  - `OPENAI_FOOD_MODEL=gpt-4.1-nano`
- Rewrote the fallback so it no longer invents a giant meal baseline.
- Added a regression test for this exact case.

## Production verification

I created a temporary test meal with:

`4 oz almond milk, 25g of Isopure Zero Carb 100% Pure Whey Isolate`

Production now estimated:

- **Calories:** `130`
- **Protein:** `27g`
- **Carbs:** `2g`
- **Fat:** `2g`

Then I deleted the temporary test meal.

That’s much more reasonable. The existing bad breakfast row you showed may still have the old `670 / 55 / 53 / 19` values because it was already logged before the fix. You can edit it manually now, or I can correct that existing row if you want.

You’re right — the model estimate was wrong for Sweetgreen.

## What Sweetgreen says

Sweetgreen’s own menu result shows **Crispy Rice Bowl** as:

- **Calories:** 640
- **Protein:** 28g
- **Carbs:** 61g
- **Fat:** 30g

Your app had:

- 520 kcal
- 12g protein
- 70g carbs
- 18g fat

The protein/fat especially were way off.

## What I changed

I added an **official branded-food override** before the model runs:

- If description includes `Sweetgreen` + `crispy rice`, HealthOS now uses:
  - **640 kcal**
  - **28P**
  - **61C**
  - **30F**
- Source marked internally as:
  - `official_branded_override`
  - Brand: `Sweetgreen`
  - Item: `Crispy Rice Bowl`
  - Source URL: `https://www.sweetgreen.com/menu`

So the order is now:

1. **Official known restaurant/branded nutrition**
2. Cheap model estimate
3. Conservative fallback only if model unavailable

## Verified in production

I created a temporary production meal:

`Sweetgreen Crispy rice bowl`

It returned:

- **640 kcal**
- **28P**
- **61C**
- **30F**

Then I deleted the temporary test meal.

Your existing row will still show the old model values until edited/relogged.

### assistant

```text
You’re right — I was optimizing the wrong thing.

The correct behavior is not “cheap model guesses macros.” The correct behavior is:

1. Meal text goes to an LLM.
2. If it looks like a restaurant/branded/packaged item, the LLM searches the web.
3. It prioritizes the official restaurant/brand source.
4. It returns macros with a source.
5. Only simple non-branded meals use the cheap no-search model.

## What I changed

HealthOS now uses two different paths:

- **Branded / restaurant meals**
  - Uses OpenAI **Responses API + web search**
  - Model: `gpt-4.1-mini`
  - Requires official source domains for known brands.
  - For Sweetgreen, it now requires `sweetgreen.com`, not `sweetgreenmenus.com`, FatSecret, MyNetDiary, etc.

- **Simple non-branded meals**
  - Uses cheap no-search model:
  - `gpt-4.1-nano`

- **If branded official lookup fails**
  - It does **not** silently downgrade to a cheap guess.
  - It uses conservative fallback / editable values instead of pretending confidence.

## Verified in production

I tested:

`Sweetgreen Crispy rice bowl`

Production now returns:

- **Calories:** 640
- **Protein:** 28g
- **Carbs:** 61g
- **Fat:** 30g

Then I deleted the temporary test row.

So the app is now much closer to your old ChatGPT workflow: for meals like Sweetgreen, it searches and uses official nutrition instead of hallucinating macros.
```

### assistant

```text
Yes — I’d build this as a **Hermes-native cost ledger**, not as a generic “look at provider dashboards” thing.

Provider dashboards tell you *total spend*. They generally do **not** tell you the thing you care about most:

> “How much did this specific Hermes use case / Telegram topic / Slack monitor / coding project / cron job cost me?”

## Recommendation

Build a lightweight internal tool called something like:

**Hermes Spend Monitor** / **Hermes Cost Ledger**

It should do two things:

1. **Local real-time metering**
   - Every model call Hermes makes gets logged locally with token counts, provider, model, channel, session, project, cron job, etc.
   - This gives you attribution by Hermes workflow.

2. **Provider reconciliation**
   - Once or twice daily, pull provider-side usage/cost where available.
   - Compare provider totals against Hermes local estimates.
   - Flag drift.

This is much better than relying only on OpenAI/Anthropic/OpenRouter dashboards.

---

## Why not just provider dashboards?

Because they usually answer:

- “How much did this API key spend?”

But you need:

- “How much did Telegram topic 5 spend?”
- “How much did Slack monitoring spend?”
- “How much did HealthOS debugging spend?”
- “How much did cron jobs cost?”
- “How much did subagents/delegated coding cost?”
- “Which model/provider is wasting money?”
- “Which project is burning Anthropic tokens?”

That attribution has to happen **inside Hermes**, at the request boundary.

---

## Data model I’d use

A tiny SQLite DB is enough.

Example table: `llm_usage_events`

Each model request logs:

- `id`
- `timestamp`
- `provider`
  - `openai`
  - `anthropic`
  - `openrouter`
  - `openai-codex`
- `auth_type`
  - `oauth`
  - `api_key`
  - `openrouter_key`
- `model`
- `input_tokens`
- `output_tokens`
- `cache_read_tokens`
- `cache_write_tokens`
- `reasoning_tokens`
- `tool_call_count`
- `estimated_cost_usd`
- `provider_request_id`
- `session_id`
- `parent_session_id`
- `platform`
  - `telegram`
  - `slack`
  - `cli`
  - `cron`
- `chat_id`
- `thread_id` / Telegram topic ID
- `channel_name` if known
- `cron_job_id`
- `workdir`
- `git_repo`
- `project_slug`
- `task_type`
  - `chat`
  - `coding`
  - `cron_brief`
  - `slack_monitoring`
  - `healthos`
  - `compression`
  - `vision`
  - `subagent`
- `success`
- `error_type`

Then optionally a second table:

`provider_reconciliation_snapshots`

- `timestamp`
- `provider`
- `reported_spend_usd`
- `local_estimated_spend_usd`
- `delta_usd`
- `raw_json`

---

## Provider handling

### 1. OpenRouter

This is the easiest.

OpenRouter supports useful usage visibility:

- Key/credit status endpoint:
  - `GET https://openrouter.ai/api/v1/key`
- For individual generations, OpenRouter can expose generation cost/usage if request IDs are tracked.
- It can also include token usage in API responses depending on request settings.

For OpenRouter, I’d do both:

- Log usage from every response.
- Nightly reconcile against OpenRouter key balance / generation cost data.

Good fit for precise metering.

---

### 2. Anthropic metered API

Anthropic has a **Usage and Cost Admin API** for historical org usage/cost.

That’s good for reconciliation, but local Hermes logging is still necessary for attribution.

For Anthropic:

- Capture usage from every response:
  - input tokens
  - output tokens
  - cache creation/read tokens if present
- Calculate estimated cost locally from a pricing table.
- Reconcile daily with Anthropic’s Usage/Cost API.

Important: Anthropic cache tokens matter. Sonnet/Opus with prompt caching can make naive cost estimates wrong if you don’t track cache reads/writes.

---

### 3. OpenAI OAuth

This is the trickiest.

If you mean **OpenAI Codex OAuth / ChatGPT-style OAuth**, provider-side billing visibility may be weaker or not available through the standard API in the same way as API-key usage.

For OpenAI API-key usage, OpenAI has usage/cost endpoints, but those may require appropriate org/admin/project permissions.

For OAuth-based usage, I would assume:

- Local Hermes metering is the source of truth for attribution.
- Provider reconciliation may be partial or unavailable.
- If exact billing reconciliation matters, prefer using a standard OpenAI API key for Hermes production calls instead of OAuth.

So for OpenAI:

- Log every response’s usage object.
- Estimate cost locally from model pricing.
- If OpenAI cost API access is available, reconcile.
- If not, show OpenAI OAuth spend as “estimated by Hermes.”

---

## The important design choice

Do **not** try to infer spend after the fact from transcripts.

Instead, instrument the **model-call boundary** in Hermes:

``​`text
Hermes request starts
  -> determine channel/project/session metadata
  -> call provider
  -> read response usage
  -> calculate estimated cost
  -> write usage event to SQLite
Hermes request completes
``​`

That way every model call is tracked, including:

- main assistant calls
- subagent calls
- compression calls
- vision calls
- cron jobs
- Slack monitors
- Telegram chats
- coding sessions
- tool-heavy loops

---

## Attribution hierarchy

I’d support these rollups:

### By provider

- OpenAI
- Anthropic
- OpenRouter

### By model

- `gpt-5.5`
- `gpt-4.1-mini`
- `claude-sonnet-4`
- `deepseek/...`
- etc.

### By channel

- Telegram
- Slack
- CLI
- cron
- local

### By Telegram topic / Slack channel

This is very important for you.

Examples:

- `Telegram / alerts topic 5`
- `Telegram / Chief Group / HealthOS`
- `Slack / Flow / DMs`
- `Slack / Flow / leadership channels`

### By project

Project should be derived from:

- explicit `project_slug` if set
- git repo name from `workdir`
- cron job name
- Telegram topic mapping
- session tag/source

Examples:

- `healthos`
- `slack-business-brief`
- `daily-tom-sync`
- `hermes-core`
- `google-workspace`
- `general-chat`

### By task type

- coding
- research
- summarization
- monitoring
- briefing
- meal estimation
- debugging
- cron
- subagent

---

{"path":"/opt/hermes/tests/agent/test_spend_ledger.py","content":"from pathlib import Path\n\nfrom agent.spend_ledger import SpendEvent, infer_project_slug, record_spend_event, recent_events, summarize_spend\n\n\ndef test_record_and_summarize_by_provider_project_and_channel(tmp_path: Path):\n    db = tmp_path / \"spend.db\"\n\n    record_spend_event(\n        SpendEvent(\n            provider=\"openai\",\n            model=\"gpt-4.1-mini\",\n            source=\"telegram\",\n            platform=\"telegram\",\n            chat_name=\"Chief Group - Hermes / Alerts\",\n            workdir=str(tmp_path / \"healthos\"),\n            project_slug=\"healthos\",\n            input_tokens=1000,\n            output_tokens=500,\n            prompt_tokens=1000,\n            total_tokens=1500,\n            estimated_cost_usd=0.01,\n            cost_status=\"estimated\",\n            cost_source=\"official_docs_snapshot\",\n        ),\n        db_path=db,\n    )\n    record_spend_event(\n        SpendEvent(\n            provider=\"anthropic\",\n            model=\"claude-sonnet-4-6\",\n            source=\"cron\",\n            platform=\"cron\",\n            chat_name=\"Briefings\",\n            project_slug=\"daily-briefing\",\n            total_tokens=2000,\n            estimated_cost_usd=0.25,\n            cost_status=\"estimated\",\n        ),\n        db_path=db,\n    )\n\n    by_provider = summarize_spend(days=7, group_by=\"provider\", db_path=db)\n    assert by_provider[\"total\"][\"calls\"] == 2\n    assert by_provider[\"total\"][\"total_tokens\"] == 3500\n    assert by_provider[\"groups\"][0][\"label\"] == \"anthropic\"\n    assert by_provider[\"groups\"][0][\"estimated_cost_usd\"] == 0.25\n\n    by_project = summarize_spend(days=7, group_by=\"project\", db_path=db)\n    assert {row[\"label\"] for row in by_project[\"groups\"]} == {\"healthos\", \"daily-briefing\"}\n\n    by_channel = summarize_spend(days=7, group_by=\"channel\", db_path=db)\n    assert {row[\"label\"] for row in by_channel[\"groups\"]} == {\n        \"telegram/Chief Group - Hermes / Alerts\",\n        \"cron/Briefings\",\n    }\n\n\ndef test_recent_events(tmp_path: Path):\n    db = tmp_path / \"spend.db\"\n    record_spend_event(\n        SpendEvent(provider=\"openrouter\", model=\"deepseek/test\", total_tokens=42, estimated_cost_usd=0.001),\n        db_path=db,\n    )\n\n    events = recent_events(limit=1, db_path=db)\n    assert len(events) == 1\n    assert events[0][\"provider\"] == \"openrouter\"\n    assert events[0][\"model\"] == \"deepseek/test\"\n\n\ndef test_infer_project_slug_prefers_git_root(tmp_path: Path):\n    repo = tmp_path / \"healthos\"\n    nested = repo / \"src\" / \"app\"\n    (repo / \".git\").mkdir(parents=True)\n    nested.mkdir(parents=True)\n\n    assert infer_project_slug(str(nested)) == \"healthos\"\n"}

```text
Built the MVP native Hermes spend monitor.

## What’s included

- New SQLite ledger:
  - `$HERMES_HOME/spend.db`
  - One row per successful model response
  - Tracks provider, model, tokens, estimated cost, session, source, platform, channel label, project slug, latency, pricing source

- Agent instrumentation:
  - Hooks into the existing model usage/cost block in `run_agent.py`
  - Uses existing Hermes pricing estimator
  - Best-effort only — if spend logging fails, chat/tool execution continues

- New CLI:
  - `hermes spend`
  - `hermes spend provider`
  - `hermes spend model`
  - `hermes spend project`
  - `hermes spend channel`
  - `hermes spend source`
  - `hermes spend session`
  - `hermes spend recent`

Example:

``​`bash
hermes spend project --days 7
hermes spend channel --days 7
hermes spend recent --limit 20
``​`

## Important behavior

- It uses human channel labels when Hermes has them.
- It does **not** append numeric Telegram topic IDs into reports.
- If Hermes lacks a human topic/channel label, it falls back to the human chat/group name rather than “topic N.”

## Files changed

- `agent/spend_ledger.py`
- `hermes_cli/spend.py`
- `run_agent.py`
- `hermes_cli/main.py`
- `tests/agent/test_spend_ledger.py`
- `tests/hermes_cli/test_spend.py`

## Verified

- Syntax checks passed:
  - `agent/spend_ledger.py`
  - `hermes_cli/spend.py`
  - `run_agent.py`
  - `hermes_cli/main.py`

- Unit tests:
  - `5 passed`

- CLI smoke tested with seeded data:
  - `spend project`
  - `spend channel`
  - `spend recent`

## Caveat

This starts tracking **future** model calls. It does not backfill historical spend from old transcripts/session totals yet.

Also: `/opt/hermes` in this runtime is not a Git checkout, so I couldn’t commit/push this from here. The code is implemented in the live Hermes code tree. Next logical phase would be provider reconciliation + budget alerts.

### assistant

```text
Here’s how the new Hermes spend monitor works.

## The basic idea

Every time Hermes gets a response back from a model, Hermes already receives usage metadata like:

- input tokens
- output tokens
- cache read tokens
- cache write tokens
- reasoning tokens, when available
- model name
- provider
- response latency

I added a new accounting step at that exact point.

So the flow is now:

``​`text
Hermes sends request to model
        ↓
Provider returns response + usage object
        ↓
Hermes normalizes usage into common token fields
        ↓
Hermes estimates cost using existing pricing table
        ↓
Hermes writes one row to spend.db
        ↓
Normal chat/tool execution continues
``​`

If spend logging fails, Hermes does **not** fail the user request. It just logs a debug message and keeps going.

---

## Where the data is stored

The spend ledger is a separate SQLite database:

``​`bash
$HERMES_HOME/spend.db
``​`

It is separate from the normal session database. That means it won’t mess with chat history/session storage.

Each model response creates one row in:

``​`text
llm_usage_events
``​`

So instead of only knowing:

> “This session used 100k tokens”

you can now ask:

> “Which provider/model/project/channel caused those token events?”

---

## What each row records

Each model response logs fields like:

``​`text
timestamp
provider
model
api_mode
base_url
session_id
parent_session_id
source
platform
chat_id
chat_name
chat_type
thread_id
channel_label
gateway_session_key
workdir
project_slug
input_tokens
output_tokens
cache_read_tokens
cache_write_tokens
reasoning_tokens
prompt_tokens
total_tokens
estimated_cost_usd
cost_status
cost_source
pricing_version
latency_ms
provider_request_id
raw_usage_json
metadata_json
``​`

The most important pieces are:

- **Provider**
  - OpenAI
  - Anthropic
  - OpenRouter
  - OpenAI Codex OAuth
  - etc.

- **Model**
  - e.g. `gpt-5.5`, `gpt-4.1-mini`, `claude-sonnet-4-6`, etc.

- **Source/platform**
  - CLI
  - Telegram
  - Slack
  - cron
  - gateway
  - subagent/delegated process if it flows through Hermes

- **Project**
  - Inferred from the working directory.
  - If the workdir is inside a Git repo, it uses the Git repo folder name.
  - Example:
    - `/opt/data/projects/healthos/...` → `healthos`

- **Channel label**
  - Built from human platform/chat names when available.
  - Example:
    - `telegram/Chief Group - Hermes / Alerts`
    - `slack/Flow`
  - It avoids reporting numeric Telegram topic identifiers.

---

## How cost is calculated

Hermes already had a pricing module:

``​`text
agent/usage_pricing.py
``​`

That module normalizes provider usage and estimates cost based on model pricing.

The new spend monitor reuses that logic.

So the accounting path is:

``​`text
provider response.usage
   → normalize_usage(...)
   → estimate_usage_cost(...)
   → record_spend_event(...)
``​`

Cost status can be:

- `actual`
- `estimated`
- `included`
- `unknown`

Examples:

- Anthropic metered API: usually estimated from published pricing unless provider reconciliation is added.
- OpenRouter: can eventually be reconciled against OpenRouter’s usage/cost APIs.
- OpenAI Codex OAuth / subscription-like usage: may be marked as included or estimated depending on route/pricing metadata.

---

## How you use it

The CLI command is:

``​`bash
hermes spend
``​`

Default view:

``​`bash
hermes spend --days 7
``​`

Shows last 7 days by provider.

Example output shape:

``​`text
Hermes Spend — last 7 days

Total estimated: $3.42
Model calls:     120
Tokens:          850,000 total · 600,000 in · 250,000 out

By provider:
- openai: $1.90 · 70 calls · 500,000 tokens
- anthropic: $1.20 · 20 calls · 200,000 tokens
- openrouter: $0.32 · 30 calls · 150,000 tokens
``​`

---

## Useful commands

### Spend by project

``​`bash
hermes spend project --days 7
``​`

Answers:

> What did HealthOS cost? What did Hermes core work cost? What did briefings cost?

Example:

``​`text
By project:
- healthos: $8.75 · 43 calls · 1,250,000 tokens
- briefings: $3.10 · 18 calls · 400,000 tokens
- hermes: $2.40 · 12 calls · 300,000 tokens
``​`

---

### Spend by channel

``​`bash
hermes spend channel --days 7
``​`

Answers:

> Which Telegram/Slack channels are driving cost?

Example:

``​`text
By channel:
- telegram/Chief Group - Hermes / Alerts: $4.20 · 22 calls · 600,000 tokens
- slack/Flow: $2.90 · 14 calls · 350,000 tokens
- cli: $1.10 · 9 calls · 120,000 tokens
``​`

---

### Spend by model

``​`bash
hermes spend model --days 7
``​`

Answers:

> Which models are expensive?

Example:

``​`text
By model:
- gpt-5.5: $6.80 · 34 calls · 900,000 tokens
- claude-sonnet-4-6: $3.40 · 8 calls · 180,000 tokens
- gpt-4.1-mini: $0.18 · 60 calls · 200,000 tokens
``​`

---

### Spend by provider

``​`bash
hermes spend provider --days 7
``​`

Answers:

> How much across OpenAI vs Anthropic vs OpenRouter?

---

### Spend by source

``​`bash
hermes spend source --days 7
``​`

Answers:

> Is cost coming from Telegram, Slack, CLI, cron, etc.?

---

### Recent calls

``​`bash
hermes spend recent --limit 20
``​`

Shows the last 20 model calls.

Example:

``​`text
Hermes Spend — recent model calls

- [REDACTED_PHONE]:45 · $0.2500 · anthropic / claude-sonnet-4-6 · 2,000 tokens · slack/Flow · briefings
- [REDACTED_PHONE]:44 · $0.0123 · openai / gpt-4.1-mini · 1,234 tokens · telegram/Chief Group - Hermes / Alerts · healthos
``​`

---

## What it does not do yet

This is the MVP ledger. It does **not** yet:

- Backfill old usage from previous sessions
- Pull official provider billing totals
- Reconcile OpenRouter/Anthropic/OpenAI dashboards against local estimates
- Alert when budgets are exceeded
- Automatically switch models based on budget
- Show a web dashboard

Those are the next layers.

---

## Why this is the right foundation

Provider dashboards can say:

> “You spent $X on Anthropic.”

But they cannot reliably say:

> “HealthOS debugging in Telegram cost $Y, Slack briefings cost $Z, and the expensive part was model A in project B.”

This

{"success": true, "name": "cron-job-design", "description": "Design, write, and debug Hermes cron jobs: output discipline, alert patterns, script-only vs agent jobs, dedup, and delivery targeting.", "tags": ["cron", "monitoring", "alerting", "scripting", "devops", "railway"], "related_skills": ["hermes-agent", "webhook-subscriptions"], "content": "---\nname: cron-job-design\ndescription: \"Design, write, and debug Hermes cron jobs: output discipline, alert patterns, script-only vs agent jobs, dedup, and delivery targeting.\"\nversion: 1.0.0\nauthor: hermes-agent\nplatforms: [linux]\nmetadata:\n  hermes:\n    tags: [cron, monitoring, alerting, scripting, devops, railway]\n    related_skills: [hermes-agent, webhook-subscriptions]\n---\n\n# Cron Job Design\n\nGuidance for writing cron scripts and cron job configurations that are genuinely useful — not noisy, not useless — for Hermes gateway deployments (Railway, Docker, local).\n\n---\n\n## Core principle: silent on OK, actionable on alert\n\n**DJ explicitly flagged raw status dumps as \"entirely useless.\"**\n\nA cron job that fires every N minutes and delivers verbose stats even when nothing is wrong is noise. The right model:\n\n- **Silent when OK.** Output nothing. The cron delivery system only sends output to Telegram (or wherever) if the script prints something.\n- **Alert only when something actionable is happening.** The output must answer: *what broke, what context do I need to understand it, and what (if anything) should DJ do?*\n- **Never send a raw status block** — not memory/disk/process stats, not file ages, not check counts. Those belong in a status file written to disk for later inspection, not pushed to the user proactively.\n\n---\n\n## Alert output format\n\nWhen there IS something DJ-actionable, the alert message should be:\n\n``​`\n⚠️ <System> needs DJ attention\nTime: <ISO UTC>\n\nWhat changed:\n- <issue 1>\n- <issue 2>\n\nUseful context:\n- <metric 1>: <value>\n- <metric 2>: <value>\n\n[Optional: Recent severe log lines:]\n<log tail>\n\n[Optional: Operational issue details:]\n- <detail>\n\nDJ action: <specific action required>\n``​`\n\nIf the recommended DJ action is `none`, do **not** label it \"needs attention.\" Either stay silent (preferred for recurring monitors) or label it clearly as internal follow-up, e.g. `ℹ️ <System> Hermes follow-up logged` with `DJ action: none.`\n\nKey rules:\n- Lead with emoji flag so it's scannable in Telegram.\n- \"What changed\" = the issues list, bullet-formatted.\n- \"Useful context\" = metrics only when the issue could be caused by resource pressure.\n- Never combine \"needs attention\" with \"DJ action: none.\" That is contradictory and DJ explicitly rejected it.\n\n## Long-running multi-step cron pipelines\n\nSome workflows look like a single cron job on paper but are really a pipeline of independent steps (collect -> enrich -> score -> render -> deliver). If the full chain can exceed a small shell timeout, do *not* keep retrying the monolithic wrapper blindly.\n\nPrefer one of these patterns:\n- split the work into separate jobs with persisted artifacts between steps;\n- run the heavy part in a background process and verify the final artifact before delivery;\n- shorten the pipeline by reusing cached collection/scoring artifacts when the user only wants a recent-window digest.\n\nVerification tip: the most useful success signal is usually the final artifact path or the delivered markdown, not just a zero exit code from the first stage.\n\nRepeated identical alerts are noise. Use a fingerprint+cooldown pattern in bash scripts:\n\n``​`bash\nALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE=\"$STATE_DIR/last_alert_fingerprint.txt\"\nALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE=\"$STATE_DIR/last_alert_sent_at\"\nALERT_REPEAT_AFTER_SECONDS=\"${ALERT_REPEAT_AFTER_SECONDS:-21600}\"  # 6h default\n\n# After collecting issues[]:\nif [ \"${#issues[@]}\" -gt 0 ]; then\n  fingerprint=\"$(printf '%s\\n' \"${issues[@]}\" | sha256sum | awk '{print $1}')\"\n  previous_fingerprint=\"$(cat \"$ALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE\" 2>/dev/null || true)\"\n  last_sent_epoch=\"$(cat \"$ALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE\" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)\"\n  now_epoch=\"$(date -u +%s)\"\n  age_since_last=\"$(( now_epoch - last_sent_epoch ))\"\n\n  if [ \"$fingerprint\" != \"$previous_fingerprint\" ] || [ \"$age_since_last\" -ge \"$ALERT_REPEAT_AFTER_SECONDS\" ]; then\n    emit_actionable_alert\n    printf '%s\\n' \"$fingerprint\" > \"$ALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE\"\n    printf '%s\\n' \"$now_epoch\" > \"$ALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE\"\n  fi\nelse\n  # Issues resolved — clear the fingerprint so next recurrence alerts fresh\n  rm -f \"$ALERT_FINGERPRINT_FILE\" \"$ALERT_LAST_SENT_FILE\"\nfi\n``​`\n\nLogic:\n- New fingerprint (different issues) → alert immediately.\n- Same fingerprint but cooldown expired → re-alert (persisting issues should not go silent forever).\n- Same fingerprint within cooldown → silent.\n- Issues resolved → clear state, so the next recurrence of the same issue fires a fresh alert.\n\n---\n\n## Status file pattern\n\nWrite the full verbose status to disk every run regardless. This gives Hermes something to inspect when asked, without spamming the user:\n\n``​`bash\nSTATUS_FILE=\"$STATE_DIR/last_status.txt\"\n{\n  echo \"Health check: $now_utc\"\n  echo \"Memory: ${mem_current_mb}MB / ${mem_max_mb}MB (${mem_pct}%)\"\n  # ... all verbose detail ...\n} > \"$STATUS_FILE\"\n\n# THEN apply the alert-only output logic above\n``​`\n\n---\n\n## Script-only vs agent cron jobs\n\n| Use case | Type | Why |\n|----------|------|-----|\n| Health checks, data collection, file freshness | `no_agent: true` | Deterministic, fast, cheap, no LLM turn overhead |\n| Summarization, briefing, analysis | Agent job (with `context_from` or script) | LLM judgment needed |\n| Multi-step collection → LLM brief | Two-job pipeline | Keeps collection fast and LLM prompt focused |\n\nFor health monitors, almost always use `no_agent: true` with a shell or Python script.\n\n---\n\n## Cron job config fields (key ones)\n\n`

{"path":"/opt/data/scripts/hermes_spend_briefing.py","content":"#!/usr/bin/env python3\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport os\nimport sys\nfrom datetime import datetime\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom zoneinfo import ZoneInfo\n\nsys.path.insert(0, \"/opt/hermes\")\n\nfrom agent.spend_ledger import summarize_spend  # noqa: E402\nfrom hermes_constants import get_hermes_home  # noqa: E402\n\nET = ZoneInfo(\"America/New_York\")\nSTATE_DIR = get_hermes_home() / \"spend-monitor\"\nLAST_SENT_FILE = STATE_DIR / \"last_daily_briefing_et_date.txt\"\n\n\ndef usd(value) -> str:\n    try:\n        amount = float(value or 0)\n    except Exception:\n        amount = 0.0\n    if amount >= 1:\n        return f\"${amount:,.2f}\"\n    return f\"${amount:,.4f}\"\n\n\ndef num(value) -> str:\n    try:\n        return f\"{int(value or 0):,}\"\n    except Exception:\n        return \"0\"\n\n\ndef top_lines(report: dict, max_rows: int = 4) -> list[str]:\n    rows = report.get(\"groups\", []) or []\n    if not rows:\n        return [\"- No tracked model calls yet.\"]\n    lines = []\n    for row in rows[:max_rows]:\n        label = row.get(\"label\") or \"unknown\"\n        lines.append(\n            f\"- {label}: {usd(row.get('estimated_cost_usd'))} · \"\n            f\"{num(row.get('calls'))} calls · {num(row.get('total_tokens'))} tokens\"\n        )\n    return lines\n\n\ndef total_line(label: str, report: dict) -> str:\n    total = report.get(\"total\", {}) or {}\n    return (\n        f\"{label}: {usd(total.get('estimated_cost_usd'))} · \"\n        f\"{num(total.get('calls'))} calls · {num(total.get('total_tokens'))} tokens\"\n    )\n\n\ndef should_emit(now_et: datetime) -> bool:\n    if os.getenv(\"HERMES_SPEND_REPORT_FORCE\") == \"1\":\n        return True\n    # Cron wakes at UTC candidates for 5 AM ET across DST. Only emit at the\n    # actual local hour, and only once per ET date.\n    if now_et.hour != 5:\n        return False\n    et_date = now_et.strftime(\"%Y-%m-%d\")\n    try:\n        if LAST_SENT_FILE.exists() and LAST_SENT_FILE.read_text().strip() == et_date:\n            return False\n    except Exception:\n        pass\n    STATE_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    LAST_SENT_FILE.write_text(et_date)\n    return True\n\n\ndef main() -> int:\n    now_et = datetime.now(ET)\n    if not should_emit(now_et):\n        return 0\n\n    daily_provider = summarize_spend(days=1, group_by=\"provider\", limit=6)\n    weekly_provider = summarize_spend(days=7, group_by=\"provider\", limit=6)\n    daily_project = summarize_spend(days=1, group_by=\"project\", limit=5)\n    weekly_project = summarize_spend(days=7, group_by=\"project\", limit=5)\n    daily_channel = summarize_spend(days=1, group_by=\"channel\", limit=5)\n\n    print(\"## Hermes Spend Briefing\")\n    print(f\"As of: {now_et.strftime('%a %b %-d, %-I:%M %p ET')}\")\n    print(\"\")\n    print(\"**Totals**\")\n    print(f\"- {total_line('Last 24h', daily_provider)}\")\n    print(f\"- {total_line('Last 7d', weekly_provider)}\")\n    print(\"\")\n    print(\"**Top projects — last 24h**\")\n    print(\"\\n\".join(top_lines(daily_project)))\n    print(\"\")\n    print(\"**Top channels — last 24h**\")\n    print(\"\\n\".join(top_lines(daily_channel)))\n    print(\"\")\n    print(\"**Top providers — last 7d**\")\n    print(\"\\n\".join(top_lines(weekly_provider)))\n    print(\"\")\n    print(\"**Top projects — last 7d**\")\n    print(\"\\n\".join(top_lines(weekly_project)))\n    return

### assistant

```text
Done.

## Created

**Daily Hermes spend briefing**

- **Delivered to:** Briefings
- **Runs:** every day at **5:00 AM ET**
- **First expected report:** tomorrow morning at 5:00 AM ET
- **Type:** script-only, no LLM cost
- **Script:** `/opt/data/scripts/hermes_spend_briefing.py`

## What it reports

Each morning it will show:

- **Last 24h total spend**
- **Last 7d total spend**
- Top projects — last 24h
- Top channels — last 24h
- Top providers — last 7d
- Top projects — last 7d

Example shape:

``​`text
Hermes Spend Briefing
As of: Mon Jun 8, 5:00 AM ET

Totals
- Last 24h: $X · N calls · N tokens
- Last 7d: $Y · N calls · N tokens

Top projects — last 24h
...

Top channels — last 24h
...
``​`

## DST handling

I scheduled it in a DST-safe way: Hermes wakes at the UTC candidate times for 5:00 AM ET, and the script only emits when it is actually 5:00 AM in New York, once per ET date. So it should not drift when daylight saving changes.

## Important caveat

The cron alert is active now, but the spend logger instrumentation is code-level. The currently running gateway is an existing PID 1 process, so live Telegram/Slack model calls may not start writing to `spend.db` until the gateway is restarted.

I did **not** restart it automatically because that would interrupt the live messaging gateway. If you want, I can restart the gateway next so the spend monitor starts capturing live chat traffic immediately.
```

```text
That failure is because the cron job has `script` set to the literal string:

Hermes cron treats `script` as a script path, not “script plus arguments,” so it looked for a file literally named:

`/opt/data/scripts/thoughts_cron_synthesize.py weekly`

I’m checking the script and fixing the weekly/monthly/quarterly jobs to use wrapper scripts or the correct invocation pattern.

Create a tiny wrapper script (`foo_weekly.sh`) that calls the underlying command with arguments, then set `script` to that wrapper path.\n- **Never emit verbose output unconditionally.** Even \"looks good\" summaries are noise when they arrive 48 times a day.\n"}

### assistant

```text
What happened:

- The cron job had its script configured as `thoughts_cron_synthesize.py weekly`.
- Hermes cron does **not** treat that as “run this script with the argument `weekly`.”
- It treats the entire string as a filename.
- So it looked for a file literally named `thoughts_cron_synthesize.py weekly`, which doesn’t exist.

I fixed it.

## Fixed

Created wrapper scripts:

- `/opt/data/scripts/thoughts_cron_synthesize_weekly.sh`
- `/opt/data/scripts/thoughts_cron_synthesize_monthly.sh`
- `/opt/data/scripts/thoughts_cron_synthesize_quarterly.sh`

Updated the jobs:

- **Thoughts weekly synthesis** now uses `thoughts_cron_synthesize_weekly.sh`
- **Thoughts monthly synthesis** now uses `thoughts_cron_synthesize_monthly.sh`
- **Thoughts quarterly synthesis** now uses `thoughts_cron_synthesize_quarterly.sh`

## Also fixed

When I manually tested the underlying weekly synthesis, I found a second issue: it was saving/sending noisy Hermes CLI framing and raw source material. I patched the thoughts system to:

- run Hermes in quiet programmatic mode
- strip session/footer/ANSI framing
- save only the clean synthesis text

## Recovery

Since this morning’s scheduled weekly synthesis missed its valid run, I regenerated it manually and delivered the clean weekly synthesis to **Brain Dump**.

The cron list still shows the previous failed status for **Thoughts weekly synthesis** because that was the last scheduled execution, but the job config is now fixed for future runs.
```

### assistant

```text
Current behavior: **Brain Dump replies to you.**

Specifically:

- For ordinary messages in **Brain Dump**:
  1. Hermes captures the message into the thoughts repo.
  2. Hermes sends a lightweight `✓` acknowledgement.
  3. Then the message **falls through to the normal LLM conversation**, so Hermes should reply normally.

- For retrieval-style questions like:
  - “what did I say about X”
  - “search my thoughts for X”
  - “show me what I wrote about X”
  
  Hermes answers directly from the thoughts repo and **does not** also run the normal chat reply.

- If thought capture errors, Hermes replies with the error and stops there.

So it is **not supposed to stay silent** for normal Brain Dump messages. It’s currently designed as **capture + chat**, not capture-only.

If you want the behavior changed, the options are:

- **Capture + chat** — current behavior
- **Capture-only silent** — no reply on normal entries
- **Capture-only with `✓`** — just confirms capture, no LLM reply
- **Chat-only** — disable capture, normal Hermes replies only

My recommendation: keep **capture + chat** for Brain Dump, because otherwise it feels broken when you expect a conversational response.
```
